    Gray’s Anatomy — Head & Neck Segment 12：眼眶與眼球、耳朵

    Nasociliary Nerve（鼻睫神經）分支

    鼻睫神經（Nasociliary nerve）在眼眶內沿內側壁前行，於上斜肌（superior oblique）與內直肌（medial rectus）之間行進，發出以下分支（圖 8.102）：

    • Long ciliary nerves（睫狀長神經）：為眼球的感覺神經，亦可能含有使瞳孔放大的交感纖維。
    • Posterior ethmoidal nerve（後篩神經）：經後篩孔穿出眼眶，供應後篩竇與蝶竇。
    • Infratrochlear nerve（滑車下神經）：分布於上下眼瞼內側、淚囊及鼻上半部皮膚。
    • Anterior ethmoidal nerve（前篩神經）：經前篩孔穿出眼眶，供應顱前窩、鼻腔及鼻下半部皮膚（圖 8.102）。

    Ciliary Ganglion（睫狀神經節）

    概述

    睫狀神經節（Ciliary ganglion）是動眼神經 [III] 的副交感神經節，與眼神經 [V₁] 的鼻睫支相連。此處為副交感神經節前與節後神經元交接之處，負責將自主神經系統的纖維傳送至眼球。

    睫狀神經節位於眼眶後部，視神經（optic nerve）外側，外直肌（lateral rectus）與視神經之間（圖 8.102）。通常接受至少兩個（可能三個）來自其他神經的分支。

    Parasympathetic Root（副交感根）

    動眼神經 [III] 下支經過睫狀神經節區域時，發出一分支至此神經節（副交感根）。副交感纖維進入神經節後，與節後副交感神經元在此交換。

    節後副交感纖維經睫狀短神經（short ciliary nerves）離開神經節，繞過視神經進入眼球後部。

    在眼球內，副交感纖維支配：

    • Sphincter pupillae muscle（瞳孔括約肌）：負責瞳孔收縮
    • Ciliary muscle（睫狀肌）：負責晶狀體調節以看清近物

    Sensory Root（感覺根）

    第二分支（感覺根）由鼻睫神經發出至神經節（圖 8.103）。此分支攜帶感覺纖維，穿過神經節後沿睫狀短神經進入眼球，負責眼球的感覺神經支配。

    Sympathetic Root（交感根）

    第三個分支為交感根，最具變異性。此分支含有來自上頸神經節（superior cervical ganglion）的節後交感纖維（圖 8.103）。這些纖維沿頸內動脈上行，於海綿竇離開血管叢，經共同腱環進入眼眶。

    交感纖維亦可能不走單獨的交感根，而是經眼神經 [V₁] 或鼻睫神經感覺根進入眼球，或直接經睫狀長神經進入。

    無論路徑為何，節後交感纖維最終抵達眼球，支配Dilator pupillae muscle（瞳孔放大肌）。

    臨床重點：睫狀神經節接受三種不同來源的纖維——副交感（收縮瞳孔）、交感（放大瞳孔）與感覺纖維。任何影響這些路徑的病變都可能導致瞳孔功能障礙。

    Eyeball（眼球）

    概況

    球形眼球占據眼眶前部。其圓形結構在前部向外突出，約占眼球總面積的六分之一，為透明的Cornea（角膜）（圖 8.104）。

    角膜之後由前向後依次為：前房（Anterior chamber）、虹膜與瞳孔（Iris and pupil）、後房（Posterior chamber）、晶狀體（Lens）、玻璃體腔（Vitreous chamber）及視網膜（Retina）。

    Anterior and Posterior Chambers（前房與後房）

    前房位於角膜後方、虹膜前方。虹膜中央的開口為瞳孔（Pupil）。後房則位於虹膜後方、晶狀體前方。

    前房與後房經瞳孔相互交通，充滿房水（Aqueous humor）。房水由後房分泌，經瞳孔流入前房，再由鞏膜靜脈竇（Scleral venous sinus / Canal of Schlemm）吸收——此為角膜與虹膜交界處的環形靜脈通道。

    臨床重點：青光眼（Glaucoma）：若房水分泌與吸收的正常循環受阻，導致液體增加，會使眼內壓升高。此病況可能導致多種視覺問題，甚至因視網膜及其血供受壓迫而失明。

    Lens and Vitreous Humor（晶狀體與玻璃體）

    晶狀體（Lens）將眼球前五分之一與後五分之四分開（圖 8.104）。為透明、雙凸、有彈性的盤狀結構，周邊附著於眼球外壁的肌肉。此附著使晶狀體能改變屈光力以維持視覺敏銳度。

    臨床重點：白內障（Cataract）：晶狀體混濁為臨床上常見的視力障礙原因。標準手術為摘除混濁晶狀體並植入人工晶狀體。

    眼球後五分之四——從晶狀體至視網膜——為玻璃體腔（Vitreous chamber）（圖 8.104），充滿透明膠凍狀的玻璃體（Vitreous body / Vitreous humor）。此物質與房水不同，無法再生。

    Walls of the Eyeball（眼球壁）

    眼球壁由三層組成（圖 8.104）：

    • Outer fibrous layer（外纖維層）：後部為鞏膜（Sclera），前部為角膜（Cornea）
    • Middle vascular layer（中部血管層）：後部為脈絡膜（Choroid），前部延續為睫狀體（Ciliary body）與虹膜（Iris）
    • Inner retinal layer（內視網膜層）：後部為視網膜的視覺部分，前部為覆蓋睫狀體與虹膜內表面的非視覺視網膜

    Fibrous Layer（纖維層）

    Sclera（鞏膜）

    鞏膜為緻密結締組織的不透明層，透過結膜覆蓋可見為「眼白」。後部有視神經穿過，多處有血管與神經分布，並為眼球肌肉提供附著處。

    眼球表面的筋膜鞘（Fascial sheath of the eyeball）覆蓋鞏膜外表面，從視神經入口延伸至角膜鞏膜交界處。

    Cornea（角膜）

    角膜位於鞏膜前方，為透明結構，覆蓋眼球表面約六分之一，使光線得以進入眼球。

    Vascular Layer（血管層）

    眼球血管層由三個連續部分組成——由後向前為脈絡膜（Choroid）、睫狀體（Ciliary body）與虹膜（Iris）（圖 8.104）。

    Choroid（脈絡膜）

    脈絡膜位於後部，占血管層約三分之二。為薄而富血管、色素的層狀結構，內鄰視網膜，外疏連鞏膜。

    Ciliary Body（睫狀體）

    睫狀體自脈絡膜前緣延伸（圖 8.104）。此三角形的結構介於脈絡膜與虹膜之間，圍繞眼球形成完整環帶，包含睫狀肌（Ciliary muscle）與睫狀突（Ciliary processes）（圖 8.106）。

    睫狀肌由縱行、環形與放射狀排列的平滑肌纖維組成，受經動眼神經 [III] 傳入的副交感神經控制。睫狀肌收縮時，睫狀體形成的環帶變小。

    睫狀突為睫狀體內表面向內突出的縱行脊狀結構（圖 8.106）。其上發出懸韌帶纖維（Zonular fibers）附著於晶狀體，共同構成晶狀體懸韌帶（Suspensory ligament of the lens）。

    睫狀肌收縮使睫狀體環帶縮小，減少懸韌帶張力，晶狀體因而變得更圓（放鬆狀態），實現看近物時的調節。

    睫狀突亦參與房水（Aqueous humor）的形成。

    Iris（虹膜）

    虹膜為血管層最前部的結構，自睫狀體向外突出，為眼的色素部分，中央有開孔（瞳孔）。虹膜內的平滑肌控制瞳孔大小（圖 8.106）：

    • 環形排列的纖維構成瞳孔括約肌（Sphincter pupillae muscle），受副交感神經支配——收縮時使瞳孔縮小
    • 放射狀排列的纖維構成瞳孔放大肌（Dilator pupillae muscle），受交感神經支配——收縮時使瞳孔放大
    Muscle Location Innervation Function
    Ciliary muscle Ciliary body Parasympathetics from oculomotor nerve [III] 收縮睫狀體，放鬆懸韌帶，晶狀體變圓
    Sphincter pupillae Circular fibers in iris Parasympathetics from oculomotor nerve [III] 縮小瞳孔
    Dilator pupillae Radial fibers in iris Sympathetics from superior cervical ganglion (T1) 放大瞳孔

    Inner Layer of the Eyeball（眼球內層）

    眼球內層為視網膜（Retina）（圖 8.104），分為兩部分：

    • 後部與側部為視網膜視覺部分（Optic part of the retina），對光敏感
    • 前部為非視覺部分，覆蓋睫狀體與虹膜內表面

    兩者交界為不規則線狀的鋸齒緣（Ora serrata）。

    Optic Part of the Retina（視網膜視覺部分）

    視網膜視覺部分由兩層組成：

    • 色素層（Pigmented layer）：緊密附著於脈絡膜，向前延續覆蓋睫狀體與虹膜內表面
    • 神經層（Neural layer）：可進一步分為多種神經成分，僅於視神經周圍及鋸齒緣處與色素層附著

    臨床重點：視網膜剝離（Detached Retina）：視網膜剝離時，神經層與色素層分離。神經層是脫離的部分，須緊急處理以保存視力。

    視網膜後表面可見以下明顯結構：

    • 視神經盤（Optic disc）：視神經離開視網膜之處（圖 8.105）。顏色較周圍視網膜淺，視網膜中央動脈的典型四分支由此向外分布。由於無光感受器細胞，為視網膜的「盲點」。
    • 黃斑（Macula lutea）：視神經盤外側的淡黃色小區域，中央凹陷為中央凹（Fovea centralis）（圖 8.105）。此處為視網膜最薄區域，視覺敏感度最高——因為此處桿細胞（rod cells，負責暗光視覺、無色覺）較少，錐細胞（cone cells，負責強光與色彩視覺）較多。

    臨床重點：光學相干斷層掃描（OCT, Optical Coherence Tomography）：HD-OCT 可取得半透明或不透明材料的 subsurface 影像，以光代替聲波產生高解析度横截面影像，對視神經及視網膜疾病的診斷與管理極有價值。

    臨床重點：視網膜前膜（Epiretinal membrane）：視網膜前膜為黃斑區域視網膜表面發生的纖維組織薄膜，可導致視覺問題。若症狀嚴重，可能需要手術移除。

    Vessels of the Eyeball（眼球血管）

    Arterial Supply（動脈供應）

    眼球的動脈供應來自多個來源：

    • 睫狀後短神經（Short posterior ciliary arteries）：眼動脈的分支，穿過視神經周圍的鞏膜進入脈絡膜層
    • 睫狀後長神經（Long posterior ciliary arteries）：通常為兩條，於視神經內外側進入鞏膜，沿脈絡膜層前行，與睫狀前动脉吻合
    • 睫狀前动脉（Anterior ciliary arteries）：由供應肌肉的动脉分支而來，隨肌肉附著於鞏膜處穿過鞏膜，與睫狀後長动脉在脈絡膜層吻合
    • 視網膜中央动脉（Central retinal artery）：穿過視神經後，於視乳頭處進入視網膜

    Venous Drainage（靜脈回流）

    眼球靜脈回流主要與脈絡膜層引流相關。四條大靜脈（渦靜脈，Vorticose veins）參與此過程，於眼球後四象限穿過鞏膜，注入眼上靜脈與眼下靜脈。另有視網膜中央靜脈伴隨視網膜中央动脉。

    臨床重點：眼底鏡檢查（Ophthalmoscopy）：使用眼底鏡可直接觀察玻璃體腔。眼底鏡為小型電池供電光源配合微小鏡片，可直接觀察玻璃體腔及經瞳孔與晶狀體可見的眼後壁。有時需使用藥物散瞳以獲得更好視野。醫師可藉此檢查視神經疾病、血管異常及視網膜變化（圖 8.105）。

    External Ear（外耳）

    耳朵為聽覺與平衡的器官，分為三部分（圖 8.109）：

    • External ear（外耳）：頭部外側的部分及向內的通道
    • Middle ear（中耳）：顳骨岩部內的腔隙，外側以膜性結構與外耳道相隔，內側經咽鼓管與咽部相通
    • Internal ear（內耳）：顳骨岩部內介於中耳與內耳道之間的一系列腔隙

    內耳將來自中耳的機械訊號（中耳接收自外耳收集的聲音）轉換為電訊號傳至腦部。內耳亦含偵測運動與位置的受器。

    Auricle（耳廓）

    耳廓位於頭部側面，協助收集聲音。由軟骨與覆蓋的皮膚組成，形成各種高低起伏的結構（圖 8.110）。

    耳廓主要結構：

    • Helix（耳輪）：耳廓外側的大環，於下方終於軟質的耳垂（Lobule）——耳廓唯一無軟骨支撐的部分
    • Concha（耳甲）：耳廓中央凹陷，外耳道（External acoustic meatus）由此深部離開
    • Tragus（耳屏）：位於外耳道入口前方、耳甲之前的小突起
    • Antitragus（對耳屏）：位於耳垂上方、與耳屏相對的突起
    • Antihelix（對耳輪）：平行於耳輪前方、較小的弧形軟骨嵴

    Muscles（肌肉）

    耳廓有多個內在肌與外在肌：

    • 內在肌（Intrinsic muscles）：於耳廓軟骨各部之間行走，可能改變耳廓形狀
    • 外在肌（Extrinsic muscles）：包括耳前肌、耳上肌、耳後肌，自头皮或顱骨延伸至耳廓，可能協助定位耳廓

    兩組肌肉皆由顏面神經 [VII] 支配。

    Innervation（神經支配）

    耳廓的感覺神經支配來自多個來源（圖 8.111）：

    • 耳廓外側較淺表面由耳大神經（Great auricular nerve，C2、C3）、枕小神經（Lesser occipital nerve，C2，後上部分）與耳顳神經（Auriculotemporal nerve，來自下頜神經 [V₃]，前上部分）支配
    • 耳廓較深部分由迷走神經 [X]（耳支）與顏面神經 [VII] 支配

    Vessels（血管）

    耳廓的動脈供應來自多個來源：頸外动脉供應耳後动脉，顳浅动脉供應耳前分支，枕动脉供應一個分支。靜脈回流沿动脉路徑。淋巴引流向前至腮腺結，向後至乳突結，可能亦引流至頸深上淋巴結。

    External Acoustic Meatus（外耳道）

    外耳道自耳甲最深處延伸至鼓膜（Tympanic membrane / Eardrum），約長 1 英寸（2.5 公分）（圖 8.112）。

    臨床重點：外耳道檢查為耳科基本診察步驟。耳垢（Cerumen）過多或外耳道感染可能阻塞通道，影響聽力及鼓膜可視性。

    Regional Anatomy Summary（區域解剖摘要）

    眼眶要點

    • 鼻睫神經為眼眶感覺的主要分支，支配角膜、虹膜、睫狀體及鼻腔
    • 睫狀神經節含副交感、交感、感覺三種根，是眼球自主神經支配的關鍵節點
    • 睫狀肌受副交感支配，負責調節；瞳孔括約肌與瞳孔放大肌分別由副交感與交感神經控制

    眼球要點

    • 眼球壁三層：外纖維層（鞏膜、角膜）、血管層（脈絡膜、睫狀體、虹膜）、視網膜層
    • 房水循環障礙導致青光眼；晶狀體混濁為白內障
    • 視網膜神經層與色素層的分離為視網膜剝離，為眼科急症

    耳朵要點

    • 耳廓由軟骨組成，負責收集聲波；其肌肉由顏面神經 [VII] 支配
    • 外耳道為聲波傳導至鼓膜的通道，長約 2.5 公分
    • 耳廓感覺神經支配複雜，來自耳大神經（C2, C3）、枕小神經（C2）、耳顳神經（V₃）及迷走神經（X）

    Gray’s Anatomy — Head & Neck Segment 13：Ear (External, Middle, Internal)

    External Acoustic Meatus

    Its walls consist of cartilage and bone. The lateral one-third is formed from cartilaginous extensions from some of the auricular cartilages and the medial two-thirds is a bony tunnel in the temporal bone.

    Throughout its length the external acoustic meatus is covered with skin, some of which contains hair and modified sweat glands producing cerumen (earwax). Its diameter varies, being wider laterally and narrow medially.

    The external acoustic meatus does not follow a straight course. From the external opening it passes upward in an anterior direction, then turns slightly posteriorly still passing upward, and finally, turns again in an anterior direction with a slight descent.

    臨床重點：For examination purposes, observation of the external acoustic meatus and tympanic membrane can be improved by pulling the ear superiorly, posteriorly, and slightly laterally.

    Innervation

    Sensory innervation of the external acoustic meatus is from several of the cranial nerves. The major sensory input travels through branches of the auriculotemporal nerve, a branch of the mandibular nerve [V3] (anterior and superior walls), and in the auricular branch of the vagus nerve [X] (posterior and inferior walls). A minor sensory input may also come from a branch of the facial nerve [VII] to the auricular branch of the vagus nerve [X].

    Tympanic Membrane

    The tympanic membrane separates the external acoustic meatus from the middle ear. It is at an angle, sloping medially from top to bottom and posteriorly to anteriorly. Its lateral surface therefore faces inferiorly and anteriorly. It consists of a connective tissue core lined with skin on the outside and mucous membrane on the inside.

    Structure

    Around the periphery of the tympanic membrane a fibrocartilaginous ring attaches it to the tympanic part of the temporal bone. At its center, a concavity is produced by the attachment on its internal surface of the lower end of the handle of the malleus, part of the malleus bone in the middle ear. This point of attachment is the umbo of the tympanic membrane.

    Anteroinferior to the umbo of the tympanic membrane a bright reflection of light, referred to as the cone of light, is usually visible when examining the tympanic membrane with an otoscope.

    Superior to the umbo in an anterior direction is the attachment of the rest of the handle of the malleus. At the most superior extent of this line of attachment a small bulge in the membrane marks the position of the lateral process of the malleus as it projects against the internal surface of the tympanic membrane.

    Extending away from this elevation, on the internal surface of the membrane, are the anterior and posterior malleolar folds. Superior to these folds the tympanic membrane is thin and slack (the pars flaccida), whereas the rest of the membrane is thick and taut (the pars tensa).

    Innervation

    Innervation of the external and internal surfaces of the tympanic membrane is by several cranial nerves:

    • Sensory innervation of the skin on the outer surface of the tympanic membrane is primarily by the auriculotemporal nerve, a branch of the mandibular nerve [V3] with additional participation of the auricular branch of the vagus nerve [X], a small contribution by a branch of the facial nerve [VII] to the auricular branch of the vagus nerve [X], and possibly a contribution from the glossopharyngeal nerve [IX].
    • Sensory innervation of the mucous membrane on the inner surface of the tympanic membrane is carried entirely by the glossopharyngeal nerve [IX].

    Middle Ear

    The middle ear is an air-filled, mucous membrane-lined space in the temporal bone between the tympanic membrane laterally and the lateral wall of the internal ear medially. It is described as consisting of two parts:

    • the tympanic cavity immediately adjacent to the tympanic membrane, and
    • the epitympanic recess superiorly.

    The middle ear communicates with the mastoid area posteriorly and the nasopharynx (via the pharyngotympanic tube) anteriorly. Its basic function is to transmit vibrations of the tympanic membrane across the cavity of the middle ear to the internal ear. It accomplishes this through three interconnected but movable bones that bridge the space between the tympanic membrane and the internal ear. These bones are the malleus (connected to the tympanic membrane), the incus (connected to the malleus by a synovial joint), and the stapes (connected to the incus by a synovial joint, and attached to the lateral wall of the internal ear at the oval window).

    Boundaries

    The middle ear has a roof and a floor, and anterior, posterior, medial, and lateral walls.

    Tegmental Wall (Roof)

    The tegmental wall (roof) of the middle ear consists of a thin layer of bone, which separates the middle ear from the middle cranial fossa. This layer of bone is the tegmen tympani on the anterior surface of the petrous part of the temporal bone.

    Jugular Wall (Floor)

    The jugular wall (floor) of the middle ear consists of a thin layer of bone that separates it from the internal jugular vein. Occasionally, the floor is thickened by the presence of mastoid air cells.

    Near the medial border of the floor is a small aperture, through which the tympanic branch from the glossopharyngeal nerve [IX] enters the middle ear.

    Membranous Wall (Lateral)

    The membranous (lateral) wall of the middle ear consists almost entirely of the tympanic membrane, but because the tympanic membrane does not extend superiorly into the epitympanic recess, the upper part of the membranous wall of the middle ear is the bony lateral wall of the epitympanic recess.

    Mastoid Wall (Posterior)

    The mastoid (posterior) wall of the middle ear is only partially complete. The lower part of this wall consists of a bony partition between the tympanic cavity and mastoid air cells. Superiorly, the epitympanic recess is continuous with the aditus to the mastoid antrum.

    Associated with the mastoid wall are:

    • the pyramidal eminence, a small elevation through which the tendon of the stapedius muscle enters the middle ear; and
    • the opening through which the chorda tympani nerve, a branch of the facial nerve [VII], enters the middle ear.

    Anterior Wall

    The anterior wall of the middle ear is only partially complete. The lower part consists of a thin layer of bone that separates the tympanic cavity from the internal carotid artery. Superiorly, the wall is deficient because of the presence of:

    • a large opening for the entrance of the pharyngotympanic tube into the middle ear, and
    • a smaller opening for the canal containing the tensor tympani muscle.

    The foramen for the exit of the chorda tympani nerve from the middle ear is also associated with this wall.

    Labyrinthine Wall (Medial)

    The labyrinthine (medial) wall of the middle ear is also the lateral wall of the internal ear. A prominent structure on this wall is a rounded bulge (the promontory) produced by the basal coil of the cochlea, which is an internal ear structure involved with hearing.

    Associated with the mucous membrane covering the promontory is a plexus of nerves (the tympanic plexus), which consists primarily of contributions from the tympanic branch of the glossopharyngeal nerve [IX] and branches from the internal carotid plexus. It supplies the mucous membrane of the middle ear, the mastoid area, and the pharyngotympanic tube.

    Additionally, a branch of the tympanic plexus (the lesser petrosal nerve) leaves the promontory and the middle ear, travels across the anterior surface of the petrous part of the temporal bone, and leaves the middle cranial fossa through the foramen ovale to enter the otic ganglion.

    Other structures associated with the labyrinthine wall are two openings, the oval and round windows, and two prominent elevations:

    • The oval window is posterosuperior to the promontory, is the point of attachment for the base of the stapes (footplate), and ends the chain of bones that transfer vibrations initiated by the tympanic membrane to the cochlea of the internal ear.
    • The round window is posteroinferior to the promontory.
    • Posterior and superior to the oval window on the medial wall is the prominence of the facial canal, which is a ridge of bone produced by the facial nerve [VII] in its canal as it passes through the temporal bone.
    • Just above and posterior to the prominence of the facial canal is a broader ridge of bone (prominence of the lateral semicircular canal) produced by the lateral semicircular canal, which is a structure involved in detecting motion.

    Mastoid Area

    Posterior to the epitympanic recess of the middle ear is the aditus to the mastoid antrum, which is the opening to the mastoid antrum.

    The mastoid antrum is a cavity continuous with collections of air-filled spaces (the mastoid cells), throughout the mastoid part of the temporal bone, including the mastoid process. The mastoid antrum is separated from the middle cranial fossa above by only the thin tegmen tympani.

    The mucous membrane lining the mastoid air cells is continuous with the mucous membrane throughout the middle ear. Therefore infections in the middle ear can easily spread into the mastoid area.

    臨床重點：Infection within the mastoid antrum and mastoid cells is usually secondary to infection in the middle ear. The mastoid cells provide an excellent culture medium for infection. Infection of the bone (osteomyelitis) may also develop, spreading into the middle cranial fossa.

    Drainage of the pus within the mastoid air cells is necessary and there are numerous approaches for doing this. When undertaking this type of surgery, it is extremely important that care is taken not to damage the mastoid wall of the middle ear to prevent injury to the facial nerve [VII]. Any breach of the inner table of the cranial vault may allow bacteria to enter the cranial cavity and meningitis will ensue.

    Pharyngotympanic Tube

    The pharyngotympanic tube connects the middle ear with the nasopharynx and equalizes pressure on both sides of the tympanic membrane. Its opening in the middle ear is on the anterior wall, and from here it extends forward, medially, and downward to enter the nasopharynx just posterior to the inferior meatus of the nasal cavity.

    It consists of:

    • a bony part (the one-third nearest the middle ear); and
    • a cartilaginous part (the remaining two-thirds).

    The opening of the bony part is clearly visible on the inferior surface of the skull at the junction of the squamous and petrous parts of the temporal bone immediately posterior to the foramen ovale and foramen spinosum.

    Vessels

    The arterial supply to the pharyngotympanic tube is from several sources. Branches arise from the ascending pharyngeal artery (a branch of the external carotid artery) and from two branches of the maxillary artery (the middle meningeal artery and the artery of the pterygoid canal).

    Venous drainage of the pharyngotympanic tube is to the pterygoid plexus of veins in the infratemporal fossa.

    Innervation

    Innervation of the mucous membrane lining the pharyngotympanic tube is primarily from the tympanic plexus because it is continuous with the mucous membrane lining the tympanic cavity, the internal surface of the tympanic membrane, and the mastoid antrum and mastoid cells. This plexus receives its major contribution from the tympanic nerve, a branch of the glossopharyngeal nerve [IX].

    Auditory Ossicles

    The bones of the middle ear consist of the malleus, incus, and stapes. They form an osseous chain across the middle ear from the tympanic membrane to the oval window of the internal ear.

    Malleus

    The malleus is the largest of the auditory ossicles and is attached to the tympanic membrane. Identifiable parts include the head of the malleus, neck of the malleus, anterior and lateral processes, and handle of the malleus.

    The head of the malleus is the rounded upper part of the malleus in the epitympanic recess. Its posterior surface articulates with the incus.

    Inferior to the head of the malleus is the constricted neck of the malleus, and below this are the anterior and lateral processes:

    • The anterior process is attached to the anterior wall of the middle ear by a ligament.
    • The lateral process is attached to the anterior and posterior malleolar folds of the tympanic membrane.

    The downward extension of the malleus, below the anterior and lateral processes, is the handle of the malleus, which is attached to the tympanic membrane.

    Incus

    The second bone in the series of auditory ossicles is the incus. It consists of the body of the incus and long and short limbs:

    • The enlarged body of the incus articulates with the head of the malleus and is in the epitympanic recess.
    • The long limb extends downward from the body, paralleling the handle of the malleus, and ends by bending medially to articulate with the stapes.
    • The short limb extends posteriorly and is attached by a ligament to the upper posterior wall of the middle ear.

    Stapes

    The stapes is the most medial bone in the osseous chain and is attached to the oval window. It consists of the head of the stapes, anterior and posterior limbs, and base of the stapes:

    • The head of the stapes is directed laterally and articulates with the long process of the incus.
    • The two limbs separate from each other and attach to the oval base.
    • The base of the stapes fits into the oval window on the labyrinthine wall of the middle ear.

    Muscles Associated with the Auditory Ossicles

    Two muscles are associated with the bony ossicles of the middle ear — the tensor tympani and stapedius.

    Tensor Tympani

    The tensor tympani muscle lies in a bony canal above the pharyngotympanic tube. It originates from the cartilaginous part of the pharyngotympanic tube, the greater wing of the sphenoid, and its own bony canal, and passes through its canal in a posterior direction, ending in a rounded tendon that inserts into the upper part of the handle of the malleus.

    Innervation of the tensor tympani is by a branch from the mandibular nerve [V3].

    Contraction of the tensor tympani pulls the handle of the malleus medially. This tenses the tympanic membrane, reducing the force of vibrations in response to loud noises.

    Stapedius

    The stapedius muscle is a very small muscle that originates from inside the pyramidal eminence, which is a small projection on the mastoid wall of the middle ear. Its tendon emerges from the apex of the pyramidal eminence and passes forward to attach to the posterior surface of the neck of the stapes.

    The stapedius is innervated by a branch from the facial nerve [VII].

    Contraction of the stapedius muscle, usually in response to loud noises, pulls the stapes posteriorly and prevents excessive oscillation.

    Summary Table

    Muscle Origin Insertion Innervation Function
    Tensor tympani Cartilaginous part of pharyngotympanic tube, greater wing of sphenoid, its own bony canal Upper part of handle of malleus Branch from mandibular nerve [V3] Contraction pulls handle of malleus medially, tensing tympanic membrane
    Stapedius Attached to inside of pyramidal eminence Neck of stapes Branch of facial nerve [VII] Contraction pulls stapes posteriorly, preventing excessive oscillation

    Vessels

    Numerous arteries supply the structures in the middle ear:

    • the two largest branches are the tympanic branch of the maxillary artery and the mastoid branch of the occipital or posterior auricular arteries;
    • smaller branches come from the middle meningeal artery, the ascending pharyngeal artery, the artery of the pterygoid canal, and tympanic branches from the internal carotid artery.

    Venous drainage of the middle ear returns to the pterygoid plexus of veins and the superior petrosal sinus.

    Innervation

    The tympanic plexus innervates the mucous membrane lining the walls and contents of the middle ear, which includes the mastoid area and the pharyngotympanic tube. It is formed by the tympanic nerve, a branch of the glossopharyngeal nerve [IX], and from branches of the internal carotid plexus. The tympanic plexus occurs in the mucous membrane covering the promontory, which is the rounded bulge on the labyrinthine wall of the middle ear.

    As the glossopharyngeal nerve [IX] exits the skull through the jugular foramen, it gives off the tympanic nerve. This branch reenters the skull through a small foramen and passes through the bone to the middle ear. Once in the middle ear, the tympanic nerve forms the tympanic plexus, along with branches from the plexus of nerves surrounding the internal carotid artery (caroticotympanic nerves). Branches from the tympanic plexus supply the mucous membranes of the middle ear, including the pharyngotympanic tube and the mastoid area.

    The tympanic plexus also gives off a major branch (the lesser petrosal nerve), which supplies preganglionic parasympathetic fibers to the otic ganglion.

    The lesser petrosal nerve leaves the area of the promontory, exits the middle ear, travels through the petrous part of the temporal bone, and exits onto the anterior surface of the petrous part of the temporal bone through a hiatus just below the hiatus for the greater petrosal nerve. It continues diagonally across the anterior surface of the temporal bone before exiting the middle cranial fossa through the foramen ovale. Once outside the skull it enters the otic ganglion.

    Clinical Summary

    Examination of the Ear

    The ear comprises three components — the external, middle, and internal ear.

    Clinical examination is carried out to assess hearing and balance. Further examination involves use of an otoscope or other imaging techniques.

    臨床重點：The external ear is easily examined. The external acoustic meatus and the tympanic membrane require otoscopic examination. An otoscope is a device through which light can be shone and the image magnified to inspect the external acoustic meatus and the tympanic membrane.

    The examination begins by grasping the posterosuperior aspect of the ear and gently retracting it to straighten the external auditory meatus. The normal tympanic membrane is relatively translucent and has a gray-reddish tinge. The handle of the malleus is visible near the center of the membrane. In the 5 o’clock position a cone of light is always demonstrated.

    The middle ear is investigated by CT and MRI to visualize the malleus, incus, and stapes. The relationship of these bones to the middle ear cavity is determined and any masses identified. The inner ear is also assessed by CT and MRI.

    Swimmer’s Ear

    臨床重點：Swimmer’s ear, often called otitis externa, is a painful condition resulting from an infection in the external acoustic meatus. It frequently occurs in swimmers.

    Surfer’s Ear

    臨床重點：Surfer’s ear, which is prevalent among individuals who surf or swim in cold water, results from the development of a “bony lump” in the external acoustic meatus. Growth of the lump eventually constricts the meatus and reduces hearing in the affected ear.

    Tympanic Membrane Perforation

    臨床重點：Although perforation of the tympanic membrane (eardrum) has many causes, trauma and infection are still the commonest causes today.

    Most ruptures of the tympanic membrane tend to heal spontaneously, but surgical intervention may be necessary if the rupture is large.

    From time to time it is necessary to enter the middle ear through the tympanic membrane. As the chorda tympani runs in the upper one-third of the tympanic membrane, incisions are always below this level. The richer blood supply to the posterior aspect of the tympanic membrane determines the standard surgical approach in the posteroinferior aspect.

    Otitis media (infection of the middle ear) is common and can lead to perforation of the tympanic membrane. The infection can usually be treated with antibiotics. If the infection persists, the chronic inflammatory change may damage the ossicular chain and other structures within the middle ear to produce deafness.

    Mastoiditis

    臨床重點：Infection within the mastoid antrum and mastoid cells is usually secondary to infection in the middle ear. The mastoid cells provide an excellent culture medium for infection. Infection of the bone (osteomyelitis) may also develop, spreading into the middle cranial fossa.

    Drainage of the pus within the mastoid air cells is necessary and there are numerous approaches for doing this. When undertaking this type of surgery, it is extremely important that care is taken not to damage the mastoid wall of the middle ear to prevent injury to the facial nerve [VII]. Any breach of the inner table of the cranial vault may allow bacteria to enter the cranial cavity and meningitis will ensue.

    Gray’s Anatomy — Head & Neck Segment 14

    Internal Ear, Facial Nerve & Temporal/Infratemporal Fossae

    圖 8.122 Greater and Lesser Petrosal Nerves 的溝槽與裂孔

    Internal Ear（內耳）

    Internal ear 由一系列骨腔（bony labyrinth）及位於其中的膜性管道與囊（membranous labyrinth）組成。這些結構均位於顳骨 petrous part 之內，介於外側的 middle ear 與內側的 internal acoustic meatus 之間（圖 8.123 與 8.124）。

    Bony labyrinth 由 vestibule、三條 semicircular canals 及 cochlea 組成（圖 8.124）。這些骨腔襯有骨膜，內含清澈液體（perilymph）。

    懸浮於 perilymph 之內但未完全充填 bony labyrinth 所有空間的是 membranous labyrinth，由 semicircular ducts、cochlear duct 及兩個囊（utricle 與 saccule）組成。這些膜性空間充滿 endolymph。

    Internal ear 的結構負責將平衡與聽覺資訊傳遞至腦部：

    • Cochlear duct 是聽覺器官。
    • Semicircular ducts、utricle、saccule 是平衡器官。

    Semicircular Canals（半規管）

    臨床重點：Semicircular canals 的病變（如骨化或囊腫）可能影響平衡功能，導致眩暈（vertigo）與失衡。

    Fig. 8.123 Internal Ear 在顳骨中的位置

    • Anterior semicircular canal（前半規管）
    • Posterior semicircular canal（後半規管）
    • Lateral semicircular canal（外側半規管）
    • Semicircular duct（半規管膜性部）
    • Facial nerve [VII]（顏面神經）
    • Vestibular nerve ganglion（前庭神經節）
    • Internal acoustic meatus（內耳道）
    • Pharyngotympanic tube（咽鼓管）

    Fig. 8.124 Internal Ear（內耳）

    Bony Labyrinth（骨迷路）

    Vestibule（前庭）

    Vestibule 是 bony labyrinth 的中央部分，其外側壁有 oval window（卵圓窗）（圖 8.125）。前方與 cochlea 相通，後上方則與 semicircular canals 相連。

    一條窄管道（vestibular aqueduct）自 vestibule 發出，穿過顳骨，開口於 petrous part 的後表面。

    Semicircular Canals（半規管）

    自 vestibule 向後上方突出的分別是 anterior、posterior 及 lateral semicircular canals（圖 8.125）。每條 canal 形成圓周的 2/3，兩端均連於 vestibule，且一端膨大形成 ampulla。三條 canals 彼此互相垂直排列。

    Cochlea（耳蝸）

    自 vestibule 向前突出的為 cochlea，是一個繞自身旋轉 2.5 至 2.75 圈的骨性結構，繞著中央骨柱（modiolus）旋轉。此結構呈錐形，底部朝後內側，頂端朝前外側（圖 8.126）。此位置使 modiolus 的寬底部靠近 internal acoustic meatus， cochlear nerve 的分支由此進入。

    Modiolus 內有一薄層骨板（lamina of the modiolus，亦稱 spiral lamina）縱貫其全长。Cochlear duct 附著於 modiolus 的 lamina 並環繞 modiolus，為 membranous labyrinth 的一部分。

    附著於 cochlea 外壁的 cochlear duct 形成兩條管道（scala vestibuli 與 scala tympani），兩者於頂端通過狹窄裂隙（helicotrema）相連：

    • Scala vestibuli 與 vestibule 相連。
    • Scala tympani 以覆蓋 round window（圓窗）的 secondary tympanic membrane 與 middle ear 分隔（圖 8.127）。

    最後，round window 附近有一小通道（cochlear canaliculus），穿過顳骨，於其下面開口至 posterior cranial fossa。此通道連接充滿 perilymph 的 cochlea 與 subarachnoid space（圖 8.127）。

    Fig. 8.125 Bony Labyrinth（骨迷路）

    Membranous Labyrinth（膜迷路）

    Membranous labyrinth 是位於 bony labyrinth 內的連續性管道與囊系統。內含 endolymph，並以 perilymph 與覆蓋 bony labyrinth 壁的骨膜相隔。

    Membranous labyrinth 由兩個囊（utricle 與 saccule）與四條管道（三條 semicircular ducts 與 cochlear duct）組成，執行平衡與聽覺的獨特功能：

    • Utricle、saccule 與三條 semicircular ducts 屬於前庭裝置（vestibular apparatus），即平衡器官。
    • Cochlear duct 是聽覺器官。

    Fig. 8.127 Membranous Labyrinth（膜迷路）

    Organs of Balance（前庭平衡器官）

    Membranous labyrinth 的六個組成部分中，有五個與平衡有關。這兩個囊（utricle 與 saccule）與三條管道（anterior、posterior 與 lateral semicircular ducts）。

    Utricle、Saccule 與 Endolymphatic Duct

    Utricle 是較大的囊，呈橢圓形，位於骨迷路 vestibule 的後上方。三條 semicircular ducts 均注入 utricle。每條 semicircular duct 形狀與其對應的骨性 semicircular canal 相似，只是小得多，一端膨大形成 ampulla。

    Saccule 是較小的圓形囊，位於骨迷路 vestibule 的前下方（圖 8.127）。Cochlear duct 注入其中。

    Utrikulosaccular duct 建立所有 membranous labyrinth 組成部分之間的連續性，並連接 utricle 與 saccule。此小管道分支出 endolymphatic duct，進入 vestibular aqueduct（穿過顳骨的管道），出現於 petrous part 在 posterior cranial fossa 的後表面。在此，endolymphatic duct 擴大形成 endolymphatic sac，這是一個硬膜外的囊，功能為吸收 endolymph。

    Sensory Receptors（感覺受器）

    功能上，平衡的感覺受器構成獨特結構，位於前庭裝置的每個組成部分中。在 utricle 與 saccule，感覺器官分別是 macula of the utricle 與 macula of the saccule；在三條 semicircular ducts 每條的 ampulla，則是 crista。

    臨床重點：Utricle 對離心加速與垂直加速有反應，而 saccule 對線性加速有反應。三條 semicircular ducts 的受器則對任何方向的運動都有反應。這解釋了為何前庭功能障礙可導致眩暈、噁心與失衡。

    Organ of Hearing（聽覺器官）

    Cochlear Duct（耳蝸管）

    Cochlear duct 位於骨迷路 cochlea 的中央位置，將其分成兩條管道（scala vestibuli 與 scala tympani）。其藉由中央附著於 modiolus 的 lamina（modiolus 的薄層骨板），以及周邊附著於 cochlea 外壁而維持在此位置（圖 8.128）。

    因此，呈三角形的 cochlear duct 具有：

    • 外壁（緊貼骨性 cochlea），由增厚的上皮襯骨膜（spiral ligament）構成；
    • 屋頂（vestibular membrane），分隔 cochlear duct 內的 endolymph 與 scala vestibuli 內的 perilymph，由含結締組織核心的膜構成，兩側覆以上皮；
    • 地板，分隔 cochlear duct 內的 endolymph 與 scala tympani 內的 perilymph，由 modiolus lamina 的遊離緣與從 spiral ligament 延伸至 cochlea 外壁的膜（basilar membrane）組成。

    Spiral organ（螺旋器） 是聽覺器官，位於 basilar membrane 上，突入充滿 endolymph 的 cochlear duct（圖 8.128）。

    Fig. 8.128 Membranous Labyrinth，橫切面

    Vessels（血管）

    Internal ear 的動脈供應分為供應 bony labyrinth 與 membranous labyrinth 的血管。

    Bony labyrinth 由供應周圍顳骨的相同血管供應——包括來自 maxillary artery 的 anterior tympanic branch、來自 posterior auricular artery 的 stylomastoid branch，以及來自 middle meningeal artery 的 petrosal branch。

    Membranous labyrinth 由 labyrinthine artery 供應，該血管或起源於 anteroinferior cerebellar artery，或直接為 basilar artery 的分支。不論其起源如何，其均與 facial nerve [VII] 及 vestibulocochlear nerve [VIII] 一起通過 internal acoustic meatus，最終分為：

    • Cochlear branch（耳蝸支），穿過 modiolus 供應 cochlear duct；
    • 一或兩條 vestibular branches（前庭支），供應前庭裝置。

    Membranous labyrinth 的靜脈引流經由 vestibular veins 與 cochlear veins，伴行於同名動脈。這些靜脈匯集形成 labyrinthine vein，最終注入 inferior petrosal sinus 或 sigmoid sinus。

    Innervation（神經支配）

    Vestibulocochlear nerve [VIII] 攜帶特殊傳入纖維，用於聽覺（cochlear component）與平衡（vestibular component）。其穿過 internal acoustic meatus 並跨越 posterior cranial fossa 後，進入腦幹外側面，介於 pons 與 medulla 之間。

    在顳骨內，於 internal acoustic meatus 遠端，vestibulocochlear nerve 分為：

    • Cochlear nerve（耳蝸神經）；
    • Vestibular nerve（前庭神經）。

    Vestibular nerve 膨大形成 vestibular ganglion，然後分為分布於三條 semicircular ducts 與 utricle、saccule 的上、下兩部分（見圖 8.124）。

    Cochlear nerve 進入 cochlea 底部，向上穿過 modiolus。Cochlear nerve 的神經節細胞位於 spiral ganglion（螺旋神經節），位於 lamina of the modiolus 繞 modiolus 旋轉的基部（圖 8.126）。Cochlear nerve 的分支穿過 lamina of the modiolus，分布至 spiral organ 的受器。

    Facial Nerve [VII] in the Temporal Bone（顳骨內的顏面神經）

    Facial nerve [VII] 在進入顳骨 internal acoustic meatus 時，與 vestibulocochlear nerve [VIII] 密切相關。穿過顳骨時，其路徑及若干分支與 internal ear 及 middle ear 直接相關。

    Facial nerve [VII] 進入顳骨 petrous part 的 internal acoustic meatus（圖 8.129A）。Vestibulocochlear nerve 與 labyrinthine artery 伴行。

    在 internal acoustic meatus 遠端，facial nerve [VII] 進入 facial canal，並在 internal ear 與 middle ear 之間繼續向外側走行。此時 facial nerve [VII] 膨大並向後外側彎曲。該膨大處為感覺性的 geniculate ganglion。Facial canal 繼續時，facial nerve [VII] 急轉向下，近乎垂直走行，通過 stylomastoid foramen（莖乳孔）離開顱骨（圖 8.129A）。

    Branches（分支）

    Greater Petrosal Nerve（大神經）

    在 geniculate ganglion 處，facial nerve [VII] 發出 greater petrosal nerve（圖 8.129A）。這是 facial nerve [VII] 的第一個分支。Greater petrosal nerve 自 geniculate ganglion 發出，向前內側穿過顳骨，通過 petrous part 前表面的 hiatus for the greater petrosal nerve（見圖 8.122）。Greater petrosal nerve 攜帶節前副交感纖維至 pterygopalatine ganglion。

    臨床重點：Greater petrosal nerve 攜帶副交感纖維至淚腺。損傷此神經可導致乾眼症（xerophthalmia），尤其在 Bell’s palsy 患者中需注意。

    繼續超越彎曲處，facial nerve [VII] 的位置在 middle ear 內側壁形成一個突起（見圖 8.121）。

    Nerve to Stapedius 與 Chorda Tympani

    在開始垂直下降處附近，facial nerve [VII] 發出一小分支——nerve to stapedius（圖 8.129），支配 stapedius muscle；就在離開顱骨之前，facial nerve [VII] 發出 chorda tympani nerve。

    Fig. 8.129 A. Temporal Bone 內的 Facial Nerve；B. Temporal Bone 內的 Chorda Tympani

    Chorda tympani 不立即離開顳骨，而是上升進入 middle ear 後壁，通過 tympanic membrane 上方區域，介於 malleus 與 incus 之間（圖 8.129B）。其然後通過一條通向 petrotympanic fissure 的管道離開 middle ear，通過此裂孔離開顱骨，與 infratemporal fossa 中的 lingual nerve 會合。

    臨床重點：Chorda tympani 攜帶味覺纖維至舌前 2/3 以及副交感纖維至 submandibular 與 sublingual glands。損傷可導致味覺減退與唾液分泌減少。

    Transmission of Sound（聲音傳導）

    聲波進入 external acoustic meatus，衝擊 tympanic membrane 使其向內側移動（圖 8.130）。由於 malleus 的柄附著於此膜，其也向內側移動。這使 malleus 的頭部向外側移動。由於 malleus 與 incus 的頭部互相關節，incus 的頭部也向外側移動。這推動 incus 的長突向內側移動。長突與 stapes 關節，其移動導致 stapes 向內側移動。反過來，由於 stapes 的底部附著於 oval window，oval window 也向內側移動。

    Fig. 8.130 Transmission of Sound（聲音傳導）

    此動作完成將大振幅、低力量的氣傳聲波（使 tympanic membrane 振動）轉換為小振幅、高力量的 oval window 振動，在 cochlea 的 perilymph-filled scala vestibuli 中產生波動。

    在 scala vestibuli 的 perilymph 中建立的波動穿過 cochlea，導致覆蓋 scala tympani 下端的 round window 上的 secondary tympanic membrane 向外鼓出（圖 8.130）。這導致 basilar membrane 振動，進而刺激 spiral organ 中的受器細胞。

    受器細胞通過 vestibulocochlear nerve [VIII] 的 cochlear part 將衝動傳回腦部，在腦中解釋為聲音。

    臨床重點：若聲音過響，導致 tympanic membrane 過度移動，tensor tympani muscle（附著於 malleus）與/或 stapedius muscle（附著於 stapes）收縮，抑制 ossicles 的振動並減少到達 oval window 的振動力量。此反射為聽覺保護機制，但在某些病變中可能受損。

    TEMPORAL AND INFRATEMPORAL FOSSAE（顳窩與顳下窩）

    Temporal 與 infratemporal fossae 是頭部外側相互連通的間隙（圖 8.131）。其邊界由骨與軟組織形成。

    Temporal fossa 位於 infratemporal fossa 上方，位於 zygomatic arch 之上，透過 zygomatic arch 與顱骨更內側表面之間的間隙與下方的 infratemporal fossa 相通。

    Infratemporal fossa 是一個楔形空間，深位於 masseter muscle 與下方 mandible ramus 之前。來往於顱腔、頸部、pterygopalatine fossa、口腔底、眼窩底、temporal fossa 與頭部淺層的結構均通過此處。

    四條 mastication muscles（masseter、temporalis、medial pterygoid 與 lateral pterygoid）中，一條（masseter）位於 infratemporal fossa 外側，兩條（medial 與 lateral pterygoid）在 infratemporal fossa 內，一條填滿 temporal fossa。

    Fig. 8.131 Temporal and Infratemporal Fossae

    Bony Framework（骨性框架）

    對 temporal 與 infratemporal fossae 邊界有重要貢獻的骨骼包括 temporal bone、zygomatic bone、sphenoid bone、maxilla 與 mandible（圖 8.132 與 8.133）。Frontal bone 與 parietal bone 的部分也參與其中。

    Temporal Bone（顳骨）

    Squamous part（鱗部）形成 temporal 與 infratemporal fossae 骨性框架的一部分。

    Tympanic part（鼓部）形成 infratemporal fossa 屋頂的後內側角，並與 mandible 的頭部關節形成 temporomandibular joint。

    Squamous part 的外表面在 temporal fossa 內側壁上有兩個表面特徵：

    • Supramastoid crest（乳突上嵴），從 zygomatic process 基部向後延伸，標誌 temporal fossa 的後下界；
    • Middle temporal artery groove（顳中动脉溝），垂直走向，為 superficial temporal artery 的分支。

    在 zygomatic process 基部下方，參與形成 temporomandibular joint 的兩個特徵是 articular tubercle 與 mandibular fossa。兩者均由內向外延伸。Mandibular fossa 之後是 external acoustic meatus。Tympanic part 是一塊扁平的凹形骨板，從 mandibular fossa 後方向下彎曲，形成 external auditory meatus 壁的一部分。

    從下方觀察，tympanic 與 squamous parts 之間有明顯的 tympanosquamous fissure。在內側，來自 petrous part 的一小片骨頭嵌入裂隙中，在其與 tympanic part 之間形成 petrotympanic fissure（圖 8.132）。

    臨床重點：Chorda tympani nerve 通過 petrotympanic fissure 的內側端離開顱骨，進入 infratemporal fossa。此通道在顱底骨折時可能受損。

    Sphenoid Bone（蝶骨）

    構成 infratemporal fossa 骨性框架一部分的蝶骨部分包括 lateral plate of the pterygoid process 與 greater wing（圖 8.132）。

    Greater wing 自蝶骨 body 向兩側延伸。它們從 body 向外側投射並向上彎曲。下表面與外表面分別形成 infratemporal fossa 的屋頂與 temporal fossa 的內側壁。

    Greater wing 外表面與下面之間的尖銳轉角為 infratemporal crest（圖 8.132）。兩個孔（foramen ovale 與 foramen spinosum）穿過 greater wing 基部，允許 mandibular nerve [V₃] 與 middle meningeal artery 分別在 middle cranial fossa 與 infratemporal fossa 之間通過。此外，一或多個小的 sphenoidal emissary foramina 穿過 greater wing 基部，在 foramen ovale 前內側，允許 emissary veins 在 infratemporal fossa 的 pterygoid plexus of veins 與 middle cranial fossa 的 cavernous sinus 之間通過。

    從 greater wing 向下垂直突出的不規則形狀結構為 spine of the sphenoid（蝶骨棘），為 sphenomandibular ligament 顱端的附著處。

    Lateral plate of the pterygoid process 是一片垂直方向的骨片，從 pterygoid process 向後外側突出（圖 8.132）。其外表面與內表面分別是 lateral pterygoid muscle 與 medial pterygoid muscle 的附著處。

    Maxilla（上頜骨）

    Maxilla 的 posterior surface 構成 infratemporal fossa 的前壁（圖 8.132）。此表面有供應 posterosuperior alveolar nerve 與血管的孔。上緣形成 inferior orbital fissure 的下界。

    Mandible（下頜骨）

    臨床重點：Mandible 的髁突（condylar process）與頸部（neck）是創傷與病理的好發部位。Pterygoid fovea 是 lateral pterygoid muscle 的附著處。Mental foramen 為頦神經離開下頜骨的位置，在局部麻醉時需注意。

    Fig. 8.133 Mandible（下頜骨）

    • Condylar process（髁突）
    • Head（頭部）
    • Neck（頸部）
    • Pterygoid fovea（翼肌凹）
    • Mental foramen（頦孔）
    • Ramus（支）

    Key Anatomical Terms（關鍵解剖術語）

    英文術語 中文翻譯

    Membranous labyrinth 膜迷路
    Vestibule 前庭
    Cochlea 耳蝸
    Semicircular canals/ducts 半規管（骨性/膜性）
    Utricle 橢圓囊
    Saccule 球囊
    Cochlear duct 耳蝸管
    Facial nerve [VII] 顏面神經
    Vestibulocochlear nerve [VIII] 前庭耳蝸神經

    Foramen ovale 卵圓孔
    Foramen spinosum 棘孔

    Gray’s Anatomy — Head & Neck: Segment 15

    Regional Anatomy: Temporal and Infratemporal Fossae

    Zygomatic Bone

    The zygomatic bone is a quadrangular-shaped bone that forms the palpable bony prominence of the cheek:

    • A maxillary process extends anteromedially to articulate with the zygomatic process of the maxilla.
    • A frontal process extends superiorly to articulate with the zygomatic process of the frontal bone.
    • A temporal process extends posteriorly to articulate with the zygomatic process of the temporal bone to complete the zygomatic arch.

    A small zygomaticofacial foramen on the lateral surface of the zygomatic bone transmits the zygomaticofacial nerve and vessels onto the cheek.

    A thin plate of bone extends posteromedially from the frontal process and contributes to the lateral wall of the orbit on one side and the anterior wall of the temporal fossa on the other. A zygomaticotemporal foramen on the temporal fossa surface of the plate where it attaches to the frontal process is for the zygomaticotemporal nerve.

    Ramus of Mandible

    The ramus of the mandible is quadrangular in shape and has medial and lateral surfaces and condylar and coronoid processes (Fig. 8.133).

    The lateral surface of the ramus of the mandible is generally smooth except for the presence of a few obliquely oriented ridges. Most of the lateral surface provides attachment for the masseter muscle.

    The posterior and inferior borders of the ramus intersect to form the angle of the mandible, while the superior border is notched to form the mandibular notch. The anterior border is sharp and is continuous below with the oblique line on the body of the mandible.

    The coronoid process extends superiorly from the junction of the anterior and superior borders of the ramus. It is a flat, triangular process that provides attachment for the temporalis muscle.

    The condylar process extends superiorly from the posterior and superior borders of the ramus. It consists of:

    • the head of the mandible (condyle), which is expanded medially and participates in forming the temporomandibular joint; and
    • the neck of the mandible, which bears a shallow depression (the pterygoid fovea) on its anterior surface for attachment of the lateral pterygoid muscle.

    臨床重點：TMJ disorder、顎關節脫位或下顎骨骨折時，特別是condylar process和neck of mandible區域受損，臨床上需評估咬合關係及下顎運動功能。

    The medial surface of the ramus of the mandible is the lateral wall of the infratemporal fossa. Its most distinctive feature is the mandibular foramen, which is the superior opening of the mandibular canal. The inferior alveolar nerve and vessels pass through this foramen.

    Immediately anterosuperior to the mandibular foramen is a triangular elevation (the lingula) for attachment of the mandibular end of the sphenomandibular ligament.

    An elongate groove (the mylohyoid groove) extends anteroinferiorly from the mandibular foramen. The nerve to the mylohyoid is in this groove.

    Posteroinferior to the mylohyoid groove and mandibular foramen, the medial surface of the ramus of the mandible is roughened for attachment of the medial pterygoid muscle.

    Temporomandibular Joints (TMJ)

    The temporomandibular joints, one on each side, allow opening and closing of the mouth and complex chewing or side-to-side movements of the lower jaw.

    Each joint is synovial and is formed between the head of the mandible and the articular fossa (mandibular fossa) and articular tubercle of the temporal bone (Fig. 8.134A).

    Unlike most other synovial joints where the articular surfaces of the bones are covered by a layer of hyaline cartilage, those of the temporomandibular joint are covered by fibrocartilage. In addition, the joint is completely divided by a fibrous articular disc into two parts:

    • The lower part of the joint allows mainly the hinge-like depression and elevation of the mandible.
    • The upper part of the joint allows the head of the mandible to translocate forward (protrusion) onto the articular tubercle and backward (retraction) into the mandibular fossa.

    Opening the mouth involves both depression and protrusion (Fig. 8.134B). The forward or protrusive movement allows greater depression of the mandible by preventing backward movement of the angle of the mandible into structures in the neck.

    臨床重點：TMJ 脫位時，下顎頭向前滑至articular tubercle前方，無法自行復位，俗稱「下巴脫臼」。

    Joint Capsule

    The synovial membrane of the joint capsule lines all nonarticular surfaces of the upper and lower compartments of the joint and is attached to the margins of the articular disc.

    The fibrous membrane of the joint capsule encloses the temporomandibular joint complex and is attached:

    • above along the anterior margin of the articular tubercle,
    • laterally and medially along the margins of the articular fossa,
    • posteriorly to the region of the tympanosquamous suture, and
    • below around the upper part of the neck of the mandible.

    The articular disc attaches around its periphery to the inner aspect of the fibrous membrane.

    Extracapsular Ligaments

    Three extracapsular ligaments are associated with the temporomandibular joint — the lateral, sphenomandibular, and stylomandibular ligaments (Fig. 8.135):

    • The lateral ligament is closest to the joint, just lateral to the capsule, and runs diagonally backward from the margin of the articular tubercle to the neck of the mandible.
    • The sphenomandibular ligament is medial to the temporomandibular joint, runs from the spine of the sphenoid bone at the base of the skull to the lingula on the medial side of the ramus of the mandible.
    • The stylomandibular ligament passes from the styloid process of the temporal bone to the posterior margin and angle of the mandible.

    臨床重點：Sphenomandibular ligament 位於 foramen ovale 附近，臨床上在进行牙科局部麻醉時（如 inferior alveolar nerve block），針頭位置過深可能觸及此韌帶或鄰近結構。

    Movements of the Mandible

    A chewing or grinding motion occurs when the movements at the temporomandibular joint on one side are coordinated with a reciprocal set of movements at the joint on the other side. Movements of the mandible include depression, elevation, protrusion, and retraction (Fig. 8.136):

    • Depression is generated by the digastric, geniohyoid, and mylohyoid muscles on both sides, is normally assisted by gravity, and, because it involves forward movement of the head of the mandible onto the articular tubercle, the lateral pterygoid muscles are also involved.
    • Elevation is a very powerful movement generated by the temporalis, masseter, and medial pterygoid muscles and also involves movement of the head of the mandible into the mandibular fossa.
    • Protraction is mainly achieved by the lateral pterygoid muscle, with some assistance by the medial pterygoid.
    • Retraction is carried out by the geniohyoid and digastric muscles, and by the posterior and deep fibers of the temporalis and masseter muscles, respectively.

    臨床重點：單側 lateral pterygoid 麻痹時，下顎會偏向患側（因健側肌肉收縮）。臨床上用於評估腦神經 V3（mandibular nerve）功能。

    Except for the geniohyoid muscle, which is innervated by the C1 spinal nerve, all muscles that move the temporomandibular joints are innervated by the mandibular nerve [V3] by branches that originate in the infratemporal fossa.

    Masseter Muscle

    The masseter muscle is a powerful muscle of mastication that elevates the mandible (Fig. 8.137 and Table 8.11). It overlies the lateral surface of the ramus of the mandible.

    The masseter muscle is quadrangular in shape and is anchored above to the zygomatic arch and below to most of the lateral surface of the ramus of the mandible.

    The more superficial part of the masseter originates from the maxillary process of the zygomatic bone and the anterior two-thirds of the zygomatic process of the maxilla. It inserts into the angle of the mandible and related posterior part of the lateral surface of the ramus of the mandible.

    The deep part of the masseter originates from the medial aspect of the zygomatic arch and the posterior part of its inferior margin and inserts into the central and upper part of the ramus of the mandible as high as the coronoid process.

    The masseter is innervated by the masseteric nerve from the mandibular nerve [V3] and supplied with blood by the masseteric artery from the maxillary artery.

    The masseteric nerve and artery originate in the infratemporal fossa and pass laterally over the margin of the mandibular notch to enter the deep surface of the masseter muscle.

    臨床重點：Masseter muscle 肥大可能導致下顎角過度突出，影響面部輪廓。臨床上咬肌厚度的超聲測量也被用於評估營養狀態。

    Temporal Fossa

    The temporal fossa is a narrow fan-shaped space that covers the lateral surface of the skull (Fig. 8.138A):

    • Its upper margin is defined by a pair of temporal lines that arch across the skull from the zygomatic process of the frontal bone to the supramastoid crest of the temporal bone.
    • It is limited laterally by the temporal fascia, which is a tough, fan-shaped aponeurosis overlying the temporalis muscle and attached by its outer margin to the superior temporal line and by its inferior margin to the zygomatic arch.
    • Anteriorly, it is limited by the posterior surface of the frontal process of the zygomatic bone and the posterior surface of the zygomatic process of the frontal bone, which separate the temporal fossa behind from the orbit in front.
    • Its inferior margin is marked by the zygomatic arch laterally and by the infratemporal crest of the greater wing of the sphenoid medially (Fig. 8.138B)—between these two features, the floor of the temporal fossa is open medially to the infratemporal fossa and laterally to the region containing the masseter muscle.

    Contents

    The major structure in the temporal fossa is the temporalis muscle.

    Also passing through the fossa is the zygomaticotemporal branch of the maxillary nerve [V2], which enters the region through the zygomaticotemporal foramen on the temporal fossa surface of the zygomatic bone.

    Temporalis Muscle

    The temporalis muscle is a large, fan-shaped muscle that fills much of the temporal fossa (Fig. 8.139). It originates from the bony surfaces of the fossa superiorly to the inferior temporal line and is attached laterally to the surface of the temporal fascia. The more anterior fibers are oriented vertically while the more posterior fibers are oriented horizontally. The fibers converge inferiorly to form a tendon, which passes between the zygomatic arch and the infratemporal crest of the greater wing of the sphenoid to insert on the coronoid process of the mandible.

    The temporalis muscle attaches down the anterior surface of the coronoid process and along the related margin of the ramus of the mandible, almost to the last molar tooth.

    The temporalis is a powerful elevator of the mandible. Because this movement involves posterior translocation of the head of the mandible from the articular tubercle of the temporal bone and back into the mandibular fossa, the temporalis also retracts the mandible or pulls it posteriorly. In addition, the temporalis participates in side-to-side movements of the mandible.

    The temporalis is innervated by deep temporal nerves that originate from the mandibular nerve [V3] in the infratemporal fossa and then pass into the temporal fossa.

    Blood supply of the temporalis is by deep temporal arteries, which travel with the nerves, and the middle temporal artery, which penetrates the temporal fascia at the posterior end of the zygomatic arch.

    Deep Temporal Nerves

    The deep temporal nerves, usually two in number, originate from the anterior trunk of the mandibular nerve [V3] in the infratemporal fossa (Fig. 8.140). They pass superiorly and around the infratemporal crest of the greater wing of the sphenoid to enter the temporal fossa deep to the temporalis muscle, and supply the temporalis muscle.

    Zygomaticotemporal Nerve

    The zygomaticotemporal nerve is a branch of the zygomatic nerve (see Fig. 8.84, p. 933). The zygomatic nerve is a branch of the maxillary nerve [V2], which originates in the pterygopalatine fossa and passes into the orbit.

    The zygomaticotemporal nerve enters the temporal fossa through one or more small foramina on the temporal fossa surface of the zygomatic bone.

    Branches of the zygomaticotemporal nerve pass superiorly between the bone and the temporalis muscle to penetrate the temporal fascia and supply the skin of the temple (Fig. 8.140).

    Deep Temporal Arteries

    Normally two in number, these vessels originate from the maxillary artery in the infratemporal fossa and travel with the deep temporal nerves around the infratemporal crest of the greater wing of the sphenoid to supply the temporalis muscle (Fig. 8.140). They anastomose with branches of the middle temporal artery.

    Middle Temporal Artery

    The middle temporal artery originates from the superficial temporal artery just superior to the root of the zygomatic arch between this structure and the external ear (Fig. 8.140). It penetrates the temporalis fascia, passes under the margin of the temporalis muscle, and travels superiorly on the deep surface of the temporalis muscle.

    The middle temporal artery supplies the temporalis and anastomoses with branches of the deep temporal arteries.

    Infratemporal Fossa

    The wedge-shaped infratemporal fossa is inferior to the temporal fossa and between the ramus of the mandible laterally and the wall of the pharynx medially. It has a roof, a lateral wall, and a medial wall, and is open to the neck posteroinferiorly (Fig. 8.141):

    • The roof is formed by the inferior surfaces of the greater wing of the sphenoid and the temporal bone, contains the foramen spinosum, foramen ovale, and the petrotympanic fissure, and lateral to the infratemporal crest of the greater wing of the sphenoid, is open superiorly to the temporal fossa.
    • The lateral wall is the medial surface of the ramus of the mandible, which contains the opening to the mandibular canal.
    • The medial wall is formed anteriorly by the lateral plate of the pterygoid process and more posteriorly by the pharynx and by two muscles of the soft palate (tensor veli palatini and levator veli palatini muscles), and contains the pterygomaxillary fissure anteriorly, which allows structures to pass between the infratemporal and pterygopalatine fossae.
    • The anterior wall is formed by part of the posterior surface of the maxilla and contains the alveolar foramen, and the upper part opens as the inferior orbital fissure into the orbit.

    臨床重點：Infratemporal fossa 是頭頸部感染擴散的重要通道。牙源性感染（尤其是上顎後方牙齒）可循此路徑擴散至 pterygopalatine fossa，甚至顱內。

    Contents

    Major contents of the infratemporal fossa include the sphenomandibular ligament, medial and lateral pterygoid muscles (Table 8.11), the maxillary artery, the mandibular nerve [V3], branches of the facial nerve [VII] and the glossopharyngeal nerve [IX], and the pterygoid plexus of veins.

    Sphenomandibular Ligament

    The sphenomandibular ligament is an extracapsular ligament of the temporomandibular joint. It is attached superiorly to the spine of the sphenoid bone and expands inferiorly to attach to the lingula of the mandible and the posterior margin of the mandibular foramen (Fig. 8.142).

    Medial Pterygoid

    The medial pterygoid muscle is quadrangular in shape and has deep and superficial heads (Fig. 8.142):

    • The deep head is attached above to the medial surface of the lateral plate of the pterygoid process and the associated surface of the pyramidal process of the palatine bone, and descends obliquely downward, medial to the sphenomandibular ligament, to attach to the roughened medial surface of the ramus of the mandible near the angle of the mandible.
    • The superficial head originates from the tuberosity of the maxilla and adjacent pyramidal process of the palatine bone and joins with the deep head to insert on the mandible.

    The medial pterygoid mainly elevates the mandible. Because it passes obliquely backward to insert into the mandible, it also assists the lateral pterygoid muscle in protruding the lower jaw.

    The medial pterygoid is innervated by the nerve to the medial pterygoid from the mandibular nerve [V3].

    Lateral Pterygoid

    The lateral pterygoid is a thick triangular muscle and like the medial pterygoid muscle has two heads (Fig. 8.143):

    • The upper head originates from the roof of the infratemporal fossa (inferior surface of the greater wing of the sphenoid and the infratemporal crest) lateral to the foramen ovale and foramen spinosum.
    • The lower head is larger than the upper head and originates from the lateral surface of the lateral plate of the pterygoid process, and the inferior part insinuates itself between the cranial attachments of the two heads of the medial pterygoid.

    The fibers from both heads of the lateral pterygoid muscle converge to insert into the pterygoid fovea of the neck of the mandible and into the capsule of the temporomandibular joint in the region where the capsule is attached internally to the articular disc.

    Unlike the medial pterygoid muscle whose fibers tend to be oriented vertically, those of the lateral pterygoid are oriented almost horizontally. As a result, when the lateral pterygoid contracts it pulls the articular disc and head of the mandible forward onto the articular tubercle and is therefore the major protruder of the lower jaw.

    The lateral pterygoid is innervated by the nerve to the lateral pterygoid from the mandibular nerve [V3].

    When the lateral and medial pterygoids contract on only one side, the chin moves to the opposite side. When opposite movements at the two temporomandibular joints are coordinated, a chewing movement results.

    臨床重點：單側顏面神經麻痺（facial nerve [VII] 損傷）時，由於對側 lateral pterygoid 功能不受影響，下顎可能偏向健側。Lateral pterygoid 功能測試要求患者向前推下巴時可觸及其收縮。

    Mandibular Nerve [V3]

    The mandibular nerve [V3] is the largest of the three divisions of the trigeminal nerve [V].

    Unlike the ophthalmic [V1] and maxillary [V2] nerves, which are purely sensory, the mandibular nerve [V3] is both motor and sensory.

    In addition to carrying general sensation from the teeth and gingivae of the mandible, the anterior two-thirds of the tongue, mucosa on the floor of the oral cavity, the lower lip, skin over the temple and lower face, and part of the cranial dura mater, the mandibular nerve [V3] also carries motor innervation to most of the muscles that move the mandible, one of the muscles (tensor tympani) in the middle ear, and one of the muscles of the soft palate (tensor veli palatini).

    All branches of the mandibular nerve [V3] originate in the infratemporal fossa.

    Like the ophthalmic [V1] and maxillary [V2] nerves, the sensory part of the mandibular nerve [V3] originates from the trigeminal ganglion in the middle cranial fossa (Fig. 8.144):

    • The sensory part of the mandibular nerve [V3] drops vertically through the foramen ovale and enters the infratemporal fossa between the tensor veli palatini muscle and the upper head of the lateral pterygoid muscle.
    • The small motor root of the trigeminal nerve [V] passes medial to the trigeminal ganglion in the cranial cavity, then passes through the foramen ovale and immediately joins the sensory part in the infratemporal fossa.

    臨床重點：Mandibular nerve [V3] 是三叉神經最大的分支，同時具感覺與運動功能。臨床上 V3 感覺區域的感覺減退或消失（如皰疹後神經痛、腫瘤侵犯）是重要的定位徵象。三叉神經痛（trigeminal neuralgia）好發於 V2 及 V3 分支分布區。

    Muscles of Mastication (Table 8.11)

    Muscle Origin Insertion Innervation Function
    Masseter Zygomatic arch and maxillary process of the zygomatic bone Lateral surface of ramus of mandible Masseteric nerve from the anterior trunk of the mandibular nerve [V3] Elevation of mandible
    Temporalis Bone of temporal fossa and temporal fascia Coronoid process of mandible and anterior margin of ramus of mandible almost to last molar tooth Deep temporal nerves from the anterior trunk of the mandibular nerve [V3] Elevation and retraction of mandible
    Medial pterygoid Deep head—medial surface of lateral plate of pterygoid process and pyramidal process of palatine bone; Superficial head—tuberosity of maxilla and pyramidal process of palatine bone Medial surface of mandible near angle Nerve to medial pterygoid from the mandibular nerve [V3] Elevation and side-to-side movements of the mandible
    Lateral pterygoid Upper head—roof of infratemporal fossa; Lower head—lateral surface of lateral plate of pterygoid process Capsule of temporomandibular joint in the region of attachment to the articular disc and to the pterygoid fovea on the neck of mandible Nerve to lateral pterygoid directly from the anterior trunk of the mandibular nerve [V3] or from the buccal branch Protrusion and side-to-side movements of the mandible

    Gray’s Anatomy — Head & Neck: Segment 16

    Temporal and Infratemporal Fossae（顳窩與顳下窩）

    Mandibular Nerve [V3] — 整體概述

    Sensory root 與 Motor root 會合後，mandibular nerve [V3] 發出小的 meningeal branch 以及 nerve to medial pterygoid，隨後分為前幹與後幹（圖 8.144）：

    • 前幹（Anterior trunk）分支：Buccal nerve、Masseteric nerve、Deep temporal nerves、Nerve to lateral pterygoid
    • 除了 Buccal nerve（主要為感覺性）之外，其餘皆為運動神經
    • 後幹（Posterior trunk）分支：Auriculotemporal nerve、Lingual nerve、Inferior alveolar nerve
    • 除了自 Inferior alveolar nerve 分出的 Nerve to mylohyoid（小運動支）外，其餘皆為感覺神經

    Meningeal Branch（腦膜支）

    Meningeal branch 起源於 mandibular nerve [V3] 的內側緣，向上穿過顳下窩，伴隨 middle meningeal artery 離開，經由 foramen spinosum 重新進入顱腔（圖 8.144）。

    臨床重點：此分支為硬腦膜（主要為中顱窩）的感覺神經，並供應與中耳相通的乳突氣房（mastoid cells）。

    Anterior Trunk（前幹）

    Nerve to Medial Pterygoid（內翼肌神經）

    Nerve to medial pterygoid 同樣起源於 mandibular nerve [V3] 的內側（圖 8.144），向下進入並供應 medial pterygoid muscle 的深面。

    在其起源處附近，有兩條小分支：

    • 一條供應 tensor veli palatini
    • 另一條向上供應 tensor tympani muscle（位於顳骨內，與pharyngotympanic tube平行的小骨管中）

    Buccal Nerve（頰神經）

    Buccal nerve 是 mandibular nerve [V3] 前幹的分支（圖 8.144）。主要為感覺神經，但也攜帶至 lateral pterygoid muscle 及部分 temporalis muscle 的運動纖維。

    Buccal nerve 穿過外側翼肌上、下頭之間，向下繞過 temporalis muscle 附著處前緣（常穿過其肌腱），進入頰部，位於 buccinator muscle 外側，供應相鄰皮膚及口腔黏膜的感覺神經，以及下臼齒的頰側齦。

    臨床重點：在口腔手術中，Buccal nerve 是下臼齒區域局部麻醉的重要目標神經之一。

    Masseteric Nerve（咬肌神經）

    Masseteric nerve 是 mandibular nerve [V3] 前幹的分支（圖 8.144；亦見圖 8.137）。穿過 lateral pterygoid muscle 上方及 mandibular notch，進入並供應 masseter muscle。

    Deep Temporal Nerves（顳深神經）

    通常有兩條，起源於 mandibular nerve [V3] 前幹（圖 8.144；亦見圖 8.140）。穿過外側翼肌上方，繞過 infratemporal crest 進入顳窩，從深面供應 temporalis muscle。

    Nerve to Lateral Pterygoid（外翼肌神經）

    Nerve to lateral pterygoid 可直接起源於 mandibular nerve [V3] 前幹，或起源於其 Buccal branch（圖 8.144）。發出後直接進入 lateral pterygoid muscle 的深面。

    Posterior Trunk（後幹）

    Auriculotemporal Nerve（耳顳神經）

    Auriculotemporal nerve 是 mandibular nerve [V3] 後幹的第一個分支，起始時有兩根，繞過上行至 foramen spinosum 的 middle meningeal artery 向後走行（圖 8.145）。

    神經路徑：首先穿過 tensor veli palatini muscle 與 lateral pterygoid muscle 上頭之間，接著穿過 sphenomandibular ligament 與mandible neck之間，彎向外側繞過下頜骨頸部，向上行於parotid gland深面，位於temporomandibular joint與耳之間。

    Auriculotemporal nerve 的終支支配大面積顳部皮膚的一般感覺，並供應外耳、外耳道、鼓膜及顳顎關節的感覺纖維。此外，攜帶來自 glossopharyngeal nerve [IX] 的節後副交感神經至parotid gland。

    臨床重點：Auriculotemporal nerve syndrome（耳顳神經症候群）可能導致臉部潮紅、出汗等症状，與此神經的副交感功能障礙有關。

    Lingual Nerve（舌神經）

    Lingual nerve 是 mandibular nerve [V3] 後幹的主要感覺分支之一（圖 8.145A,B）。攜帶舌頭前三分之二、口腔底部黏膜及下齒相關齦的一般感覺。

    Lingual nerve 在顳下窩高處與 facial nerve [VII] 的 chorda tympani 會合（圖 8.145C），後者攜帶：

    • 舌頭前三分之二的味覺
    • 口腔裂以下所有唾液腺的副交感神經纖維

    Lingual nerve 先下降於 tensor veli palatini muscle 與 lateral pterygoid muscle 之間（在此與 chorda tympani 會合），隨後下降跨過 medial pterygoid muscle 外側面進入口腔。

    Lingual nerve 進入口腔時位於 mylohyoid muscle 後附著處至 pterygomandibular raphe（上咽縮肌附著處）之間。進入口腔底時，神經位於下頜骨內面、最后一顆臼齒正下方的淺溝中。此位置可通過口腔黏膜觸及，在臼齒及齦手術時有受損風險（圖 8.145C）。

    Lingual nerve 進入舌頭時位於 hyoglossus muscle 外側面，並在此處與 submandibular ganglion（下頜下神經節）相連。

    臨床重點：Lingual nerve injury

    Lingual nerve injury（舌神經損傷）：

    • 若損傷部位在 chorda tympani 會合點近端（proximal），會導致舌頭前三分之二、口腔黏膜、齦部、下唇及下巴的一般感覺喪失。
    • 若損傷部位在 chorda tympani 會合點遠端（distal），則 additionally 會喪失口腔裂以下所有唾液腺的分泌功能以及舌頭前三分之二的味覺。

    Inferior Alveolar Nerve（下齒槽神經）

    Inferior alveolar nerve 與 lingual nerve 同樣是 mandibular nerve [V3] 後幹的主要感覺分支（圖 8.145A-C）。除了支配所有下齒及相關齦的大部分區域外，還供應下唇黏膜與皮膚，以及下巴的皮膚。有一條運動分支支配 mylohyoid muscle 及 digastric muscle 的前腹。

    Inferior alveolar nerve 起點在 lateral pterygoid muscle 深面，與 lingual nerve 同時起源於 mandibular nerve [V3] 後幹。向下跨過 medial pterygoid muscle 外側面，經過 sphenomandibular ligament 與下頜支之間，通過 mandibular foramen 進入mandibular canal。

    就在進入 mandibular foramen 之前，發出 nerve to mylohyoid（圖 8.145C），此神經位於孔下方之 mylohyoid groove，向前進入口腔底下方，供應 mylohyoid muscle 與 digastric muscle 前腹。

    Inferior alveolar nerve 在下頜骨內向前走行。Mandibular canal 及其內容物位於臼齒根部下方，根部偶爾彎曲繞過此管道，使牙齒拔除困難。

    Inferior alveolar nerve 供應三顆臼齒及第二前臼齒及其唇側齦，隨後分為兩條終支：

    • Incisive nerve：繼續在 mandibular canal 內走行，供應第一前臼齒、門齒及犬齒及相關齦
    • Mental nerve：通過 mental foramen 離開下頜骨，供應下唇及下巴（圖 8.145A,B）。Mental nerve 可在第一前臼齒根部旁的口腔黏膜處觸及，有時可見

    臨床重點：Dental anesthesia

    Inferior alveolar nerve 麻醉是牙科最廣泛執行的局部麻醉之一。此神經是 mandibular nerve [V3] 最大的分支之一，攜帶牙齒及下頜骨的感覺分支，並接收大部分下頜骨皮膚的感覺資訊。

    麻醉操作：針頭置於口腔內，fauces 前弓（palatoglossal arch）外側，沿下頜支內側面向下頜支下三分之一的前緣前進，使麻醉劑沉積於此區域。也可根據需要麻醉infra-orbital nerve 及 buccal nerve。

    Chorda Tympani 與 Lesser Petrosal Nerve

    兩條腦神經的分支在顳下窩加入 mandibular nerve [V3] 的分支（圖 8.146）：

    • Chorda tympani（來自 facial nerve [VII]）
    • Lesser petrosal nerve（來自中耳的 tympanic plexus，起源於 glossopharyngeal nerve [IX] 的分支）（亦見圖 8.121，p.964）

    Chorda Tympani（鼓索神經）

    Chorda tympani（圖 8.146）攜帶舌頭前三分之二的味覺以及口腔裂以下所有唾液腺的副交感神經支配。

    Chorda tympani 起源於顳骨內的 facial nerve [VII]，與中耳乳突壁相鄰，穿行於小管道中，進入中耳外側面。繼續向前上跨過中耳時，與錘骨柄相隔。通過 petrotympanic fissure 內端離開中耳，進入顳下窩，先後下降於蝶骨棘內側及外側翼肌外側，會合於 lingual nerve。

    由 chorda tympani 攜帶的節前副交感纖維，與submandibular ganglion（下頜下神經節）中的節後副交感纖維形成突觸。Submandibular ganglion「懸掛」於口腔底部的 lingual nerve 上（圖 8.146）。

    節後副交感纖維離開 submandibular ganglion：

    • 重新進入 lingual nerve，隨其終支到達目標組織，或
    • 直接從 submandibular ganglion 進入腺體（圖 8.146）

    味覺（SA）纖維不經過神經節，隨 lingual nerve 終支分布。

    Lesser Petrosal Nerve（小岩部神經）

    Lesser petrosal nerve 主要攜帶至 parotid gland 的副交感纖維（圖 8.146）。節前副交感纖維位於 glossopharyngeal nerve [IX] 離開 jugular foramen 時。Tympanic nerve（圖 8.146B）在 jugular foramen 內或其出口處自 glossopharyngeal nerve [IX] 分出。

    Tympanic nerve 重新進入顳骨，通過位於 jugular foramen 與 carotid canal 之間骨脊上的小孔，通過小骨管（inferior tympanic canaliculus）上升至中耳迷路壁（內側壁）上的promontory，參與形成 tympanic plexus。Lesser petrosal nerve 即為此神經叢的分支（圖 8.146B）。

    Lesser petrosal nerve 主要含節前副交感纖維，離開中耳後進入中顱窩，通過顳骨岩部前面的小孔（比 greater petrosal nerve 開口的外側和下方），向內側走行，通過 foramen ovale 與 mandibular nerve [V3] 一起下降。

    在顳下窩，節前副交感纖維與位於 mandibular nerve [V3] 內側緣（在 nerve to medial pterygoid 起源處周圍）的 otic ganglion 中的節後副交感纖維細胞體形成突觸。節後副交感纖維離開 otic ganglion，加入 auriculotemporal nerve，攜帶至parotid gland。

    Maxillary Artery（上頜動脈）

    Maxillary artery 是頸部 external carotid artery 最大的分支，是鼻腔、口腔側壁與頂壁、所有牙齒及顱內硬腦膜血液供應的主要來源。穿過顳下窩並供應該區，隨後通過 pterygomaxillary fissure 進入pterygopalatine fossa，發出終支（圖 8.147）。

    Maxillary artery 起點位於 parotid gland 內，通過下頜骨頸部與 sphenomandibular ligament 之間向前進入顳下窩。斜向上穿過顳下窩，通過 pterygomaxillary fissure 進入翼顎窩。血管此段可位於 lateral pterygoid 下頭的外側或內側。若位於下頭內側，則在上、下頭之間向外彎曲至 pterygomaxillary fissure。

    Branches（分支）

    Maxillary artery 的分支如下（圖 8.147）：

    • 第一段（下頜骨頸部與 sphenomandibular ligament 之間）：發出兩條主要分支（middle meningeal artery、inferior alveolar artery）及數條小分支（deep auricular artery、anterior tympanic artery、accessory meningeal artery）
    • 第二段（與 lateral pterygoid muscle 相關）：發出 deep temporal arteries、masseteric artery、buccal artery、pterygoid branches，與 mandibular nerve [V3] 的分支伴行
    • 第三段：位於 pterygopalatine fossa（見圖 8.154）

    Middle Meningeal Artery（腦膜中動脈）

    Middle meningeal artery 垂直上行自 maxillary artery，穿過 foramen spinosum 進入顱腔（圖 8.147）。在顳下窩，於 sphenomandibular ligament（內側）與 lateral pterygoid muscle（外側）之間上行。就在 foramen spinosum 下方，穿過 auriculotemporal nerve 兩根之間（圖 8.147）。

    Middle meningeal artery 是腦膜血管中最大者，供應硬腦膜、顱壁骨頭及其骨髓。

    臨床重點：顱腔內，middle meningeal artery 及其分支行走於緊密附著於骨壁的硬腦膜骨膜層（外層）中。當頭部受到側方撞擊時，middle meningeal artery 的主要分支可受損。血管撕裂時，滲出的血液在动脉壓下慢慢將硬腦膜從骨附著處分開，形成硬膜外血腫（extradural hematoma）。

    Inferior Alveolar Artery（下齒槽動脈）

    Inferior alveolar artery 下降自 maxillary artery，與 inferior alveolar nerve 一起通過 mandibular foramen 進入下頜管（圖 8.147）。分布與 inferior alveolar nerve 相同，供應所有下齒，並對頰側齦、下巴及下唇有所貢獻。進入下頜骨之前，發出小的 mylohyoid branch，伴行 nerve to mylohyoid。

    Deep Auricular、Anterior Tympanic、Accessory Meningeal Arteries

    這些小分支起源於 maxillary artery 第一段，分別供應外耳道、鼓膜深面及顱內硬腦膜。Accessory meningeal branch 在上升穿過 foramen ovale 進入顱腔之前，也對顳下窩周圍肌肉發出小分支。

    第二段分支

    • Deep temporal arteries（通常兩條）：起源於第二段，與 deep temporal nerves 伴行，供應顳窩中的 temporalis muscle（圖 8.147）
    • Pterygoid arteries（多條）：供應翼肌
    • Masseteric artery：伴 masseteric nerve 穿過 mandibular notch 供應 masseter muscle
    • Buccal artery：伴 buccal nerve 分布，供應頰部皮膚、肌肉及口腔黏膜

    Pterygoid Plexus（翼狀靜脈叢）

    Pterygoid plexus 是位於內、外翼肌之間，以及 lateral pterygoid 與 temporalis 之間的靜脈網（圖 8.148）。

    引流與臨床意義

    由顳下窩及翼顎窩中 maxillary artery 分支所供應的各區域，其靜脈皆引流至 pterygoid plexus。這些屬支包括：鼻腔、口腔頂及側壁、所有牙齒、顳下窩肌肉、鼻竇及鼻咽的引流靜脈。此外，來眼眶的 inferior ophthalmic vein 可經下眶裂引流至 pterygoid plexus。

    臨床重點：Emissary veins（導靜脈）常連接顳下窩的 pterygoid plexus 與顱腔內的 cavernous sinus（海綿竇）。這些導靜脈穿過 foramen ovale、充滿於 foramen lacerum 的軟骨，以及蝶骨翼突內側板根部的一個小 sphenoidal foramen，是感染從牙齒等被 pterygoid plexus 引流的結構擴散至顱腔的途徑。此外，由於頭頸部靜脈沒有瓣膜，當麻醉劑被壓力注入翼狀靜脈叢的靜脈時，可能倒流進入組織或顱腔。

    Pterygoid plexus 連接：

    • 後方：經短 maxillary vein 與頸部的 retromandibular vein 相連
    • 前方：經 deep facial vein 與面部的 facial vein 相連

    Pterygopalatine Fossa（翼顎窩）

    Pterygopalatine fossa 是位於顱骨側面、上頜骨後方的一個倒置淚滴狀空間（圖 8.149）。

    雖然體積小，但 pterygopalatine fossa 透過裂隙及孔道與以下結構相通：

    • 中顱窩（middle cranial fossa）
    • 顳下窩（infratemporal fossa）
    • 眼眶底部（floor of the orbit）
    • 鼻腔外側壁（lateral wall of the nasal cavity）
    • 口咽（oropharynx）
    • 口腔頂（roof of the oral cavity）

    由於其戰略位置，pterygopalatine fossa 是 maxillary nerve [V2] 及 maxillary artery 終段的分布主要部位。其中也含有 pterygopalatine ganglion，來自 facial nerve [VII] 的節前副交感纖維在此與節後副交感纖維形成突觸，並與來自 T1 脊髓段的交感纖維一起加入 maxillary nerve [V2] 的分支。

    所有上齒的支配神經及血液供應皆來自穿過 pterygopalatine fossa 的 maxillary nerve [V2] 及 maxillary artery 終段。

    Skeletal Framework（骨骼框架）

    Pterygopalatine fossa 的壁由腭骨、上頜骨及蝶骨的部分組成（圖 8.149）：

    • 前壁：上頜骨後面
    • 內壁：腭骨外側面
    • 後壁及頂壁：蝶骨部分

    構成 pterygopalatine fossa 的蝶骨部分是**翼突（pterygoid process）**的前上表面（圖 8.150）。此表面有兩個大孔：

    • Foramen rotundum（圓孔）— maxillary nerve [V2] 由此通過
    • Pterygoid canal（翼管）— 含有來自 greater petrosal nerve 及 deep petrosal nerve 的交感纖維

    Gray’s Anatomy — Head & Neck, Segment 17

    Pterygopalatine Fossa（翼突窩）

    Regional Anatomy — Pterygopalatine Fossa

    The maxillary nerve [V₂] passes through the most lateral and superior of these—the foramen rotundum—which communicates posteriorly with the middle cranial fossa (Fig. 8.150B).

    The greater petrosal nerve from the facial nerve [VII] and sympathetic fibers from the internal carotid plexus join to form the nerve of the pterygoid canal that passes forward into the pterygopalatine fossa through the more medial and inferior foramen—the anterior opening of the pterygoid canal.

    Pterygoid Canal（翼管）

    The pterygoid canal (Fig. 8.150A) is a bony canal running horizontally through the root of the pterygoid process of the sphenoid bone. It opens anteriorly into the pterygopalatine fossa. Posteriorly it continues through the cartilage filling the foramen lacerum and opens into the middle cranial fossa just anteroinferior to the internal carotid artery as the vessel enters the cranial cavity through the carotid canal (Fig. 8.150B).

    臨床重點：翼管是翼突窩與顱中窩之間的重要通道。臨床上，鼻內鏡手術中若損傷此區域，可能影響翼管神經，導致面部感覺異常或腺體分泌障礙。

    Gateways（通道）

    Seven foramina and fissures provide apertures through which structures enter and leave the pterygopalatine fossa (Fig. 8.151):

    • The foramen rotundum and pterygoid canal communicate with the middle cranial fossa and open onto the posterior wall.
    • A small palatovaginal canal opens onto the posterior wall and leads to the nasopharynx.
    • The palatine canal leads to the roof of the oral cavity (hard palate) and opens inferiorly.
    • The sphenopalatine foramen opens onto the lateral wall of the nasal cavity and is in the medial wall.
    • The lateral aspect of the pterygopalatine fossa is continuous with the infratemporal fossa via a large gap (the pterygomaxillary fissure) between the posterior surface of the maxilla and pterygoid process of the sphenoid bone.
    • The superior aspect of the anterior wall of the fossa opens into the floor of the orbit via the inferior orbital fissure.

    Contents（內容物）

    The maxillary nerve [V₂] and terminal part of the maxillary artery enter and branch within the pterygopalatine fossa. In addition, the nerve of the pterygoid canal enters the fossa carrying:

    • preganglionic parasympathetic fibers from the greater petrosal branch of the facial nerve [VII]
    • postganglionic sympathetic fibers from the deep petrosal branch of the carotid plexus

    The preganglionic parasympathetic fibers synapse in the pterygopalatine ganglion and both the sympathetic and postganglionic parasympathetic fibers pass with branches of the maxillary nerve [V₂] out of the fossa and into adjacent regions.

    In addition to nerves and arteries, veins and lymphatics also pass through the pterygopalatine fossa.

    臨床重點：翼突窩是顱外神經血管進入顱內及面部各區域的樞紐。臨床上，翼突窩病變（如腫瘤或感染）可同時影響眼眶、鼻腔、口腔及顱底結構。

    Maxillary Nerve [V₂]（上頜神經）

    The maxillary nerve [V₂] is purely sensory. It originates from the trigeminal ganglion in the cranial cavity, exits the middle cranial fossa, and enters the pterygopalatine fossa through the foramen rotundum (Fig. 8.152). It passes anteriorly through the fossa and exits as the infra-orbital nerve through the inferior orbital fissure.

    While passing through the pterygopalatine fossa, the maxillary nerve [V₂] gives rise to the zygomatic nerve, the posterior superior alveolar nerve, and two ganglionic branches (Fig. 8.152). The two ganglionic branches originate from its inferior surface and pass through the pterygopalatine ganglion.

    Postganglionic parasympathetic fibers, arising in the pterygopalatine ganglion, join the general sensory branches of the maxillary nerve [V₂] in the pterygopalatine ganglion, as do postganglionic sympathetic fibers from the carotid plexus. The three types of fibers leave the ganglion as orbital, palatine, nasal, and pharyngeal branches.

    Branches（分支）

    Orbital Branches（眼眶支）

    The orbital branches are small and pass through the inferior orbital fissure to contribute to the supply of the orbital wall and of the sphenoidal and ethmoidal sinuses.

    Greater and Lesser Palatine Nerves（腭大神經與腭小神經）

    The greater and lesser palatine nerves (Fig. 8.152) pass inferiorly from the pterygopalatine ganglion, enter and pass through the palatine canal, and enter the oral surface of the palate through the greater and lesser palatine foramina.

    The greater palatine nerve passes forward on the roof of the oral cavity to innervate mucosa and glands of the hard palate and the adjacent gingiva, almost as far forward as the incisor teeth.

    In the palatine canal, the greater palatine nerve gives origin to posterior inferior nasal nerves, which pass medially through small foramina in the perpendicular plate of the palatine bone and contribute to the innervation of the lateral nasal wall.

    After passing through the lesser palatine foramen, the lesser palatine nerve passes posteriorly to supply the soft palate.

    臨床重點：腭大神經阻滯麻醉常用於上頜骨手術。注射部位若偏離，可因神經分布重疊而效果不佳，亦可能影響腭部腺體分泌。

    Nasal Nerves（鼻神經）

    The nasal nerves (Fig. 8.152), approximately seven in number, pass medially through the sphenopalatine foramen to enter the nasal cavity. Most pass anteriorly to supply the lateral wall of the nasal cavity, while others pass across the roof to supply the medial wall.

    One of the nerves passing across the roof to supply the medial wall of the nasal cavity (the nasopalatine nerve) is the largest of the nasal nerves and passes anteriorly down the nasal septum, through the incisive canal and fossa in the hard palate to enter the roof of the oral cavity and supply mucosa, gingiva, and glands adjacent to the incisor teeth.

    Pharyngeal Nerve（咽神經）

    The pharyngeal nerve (Fig. 8.152) passes posteriorly from the pterygopalatine ganglion, and leaves the fossa through the palatovaginal canal, which it then exits to supply the mucosa and glands of the nasopharynx.

    Zygomatic Nerve（顴神經）

    The zygomatic nerve (Fig. 8.152) originates directly from the maxillary nerve [V₂] in the pterygopalatine fossa, which it leaves to enter the orbit through the inferior orbital fissure. It passes forward on the lateral orbital wall and divides into:

    • zygomaticotemporal branch — continues forward at the base of the lateral orbital wall, passes through a small bony canal in the zygomatic bone to enter the temporal fossa through a small foramen in the lateral orbital margin on the posterior surface of the frontal process of the zygomatic bone, and passes superficially to supply skin over the temple.
    • zygomaticofacial branch — also passes forward at the base of the lateral orbital wall and leaves through a small bony canal, in the orbital margin, which opens via multiple small foramina on the anterolateral surface of the zygomatic bone, and its branches supply the adjacent skin.

    Posterior Superior Alveolar Nerve（後上牙槽神經）

    The posterior superior alveolar nerve (Fig. 8.152) originates from the maxillary nerve [V₂] in the pterygopalatine fossa and passes laterally out of the fossa through the pterygomaxillary fissure to enter the infratemporal fossa. It continues laterally and inferiorly to enter the posterior surface of the maxilla through a small alveolar foramen approximately midway between the last molar tooth and the inferior orbital fissure. It then passes inferiorly just deep to the mucosa of the maxillary sinus to join the superior dental plexus.

    The posterior superior alveolar nerve supplies the molar teeth and adjacent buccal gingivae, and contributes to the supply of the maxillary sinus.

    Infra-orbital Nerve（眶下神經）

    The infra-orbital nerve (Fig. 8.152) is the anterior continuation of the maxillary nerve [V₂] that leaves the pterygopalatine fossa through the inferior orbital fissure. It lies first in the infra-orbital groove in the floor of the orbit and then continues forward in the infra-orbital canal.

    While in the infra-orbital groove and canal, the infra-orbital nerve gives origin to middle and anterior superior alveolar nerves, respectively, which ultimately join the superior alveolar plexus to supply the upper teeth:

    • The middle superior alveolar nerve also supplies the maxillary sinus.
    • The anterior superior alveolar nerve also gives origin to a small nasal branch, which passes medially through the lateral wall of the nasal cavity to supply parts of the areas of the nasal floor and walls.

    The infra-orbital nerve exits the infra-orbital canal through the infra-orbital foramen inferior to the orbital margin and divides into nasal, palpebral, and superior labial branches:

    • Nasal branches — supply skin over the lateral aspect of the external nose and part of the nasal septum.
    • Palpebral branches — supply skin of the lower eyelid.
    • Superior labial branches — supply skin over the cheek and upper lip, and the related oral mucosa.
